People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s noticeably slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. Even answering texts feels like a lot, and I hate admitting that because it sounds bad, but it’s like… the pleasure isn’t really there enough to make it feel worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any issues with your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights feel better than others. I’m still getting to work, so I guess it’s not completely out of control or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I know I should be eating regularly, especially with working at the clinic and everything. But most days food just doesn’t sound that good. I’ll get halfway through a shift and realize I’ve only had coffee or maybe some crackers all day. I know that’s not great… I’m trying to be better about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I mean, I don’t want to scare anyone, and I’m not saying I have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment was that I’ve had thoughts like, I wish I just wouldn’t wake up. Or like I could just not be here for a while. I know that sounds awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m speeding up, no. If anything it’s kind of the opposite… like everything takes more effort than it should. It’s embarrassing, because I know I’m capable of doing it faster. I do feel bad about not keeping up the way I think I should, but I try not to go down that road too much. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, I don’t think. It’s more… slowed down, if that makes sense. Like for the past few weeks there’s been this heavy sadness or pressure sitting on me most of the day. I’m still going to work and trying to smile with patients and do what I’m supposed to do, but inside I feel like I’m barely keeping it together. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself enjoying things you typically like to do, or has that been lacking lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been lacking, I think. I mean, I still tell myself I could do things, but after my shifts I used to paint or go to yoga, or sometimes salsa, just to unwind. Lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch, and I’ll ignore texts because even replying or going out feels like too much effort. I feel kind of bad saying that, because it sounds lazy, but it’s more like… none of it feels worth it right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in the things you do, even if you're not motivated to do them?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can still sort of imagine that painting or yoga or salsa should feel nice, and I used to do those after shifts to decompress. But lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch. Even answering texts feels like a lot, and I hate admitting that because it sounds bad, but it’s like… the pleasure isn’t really there enough to make it feel worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any issues with your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights feel better than others. I’m still getting to work, so I guess it’s not completely out of control or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I know I should be eating regularly, especially with working at the clinic and everything. But most days food just doesn’t sound that good. I’ll get halfway through a shift and realize I’ve only had coffee or maybe some crackers all day. I know that’s not great… I’m trying to be better about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I mean, I don’t want to scare anyone, and I’m not saying I have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment was that I’ve had thoughts like, I wish I just wouldn’t wake up. Or like I could just not be here for a while. I know that sounds awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m speeding up, no. If anything it’s kind of the opposite… like everything takes more effort than it should. I’m still doing what I need to do, but at the clinic even simple stuff—getting wound care supplies together, finishing charting—feels like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s embarrassing, because I know I’m capable of doing it faster. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say worthless. That sounds so harsh. I do feel bad about not keeping up the way I think I should, but I try not to go down that road too much. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, I don’t think. It’s more… slowed down, if that makes sense. Like everything I do takes longer than it should. Even at the clinic, simple things like gathering wound supplies or finishing charting just feel like I’m moving through syrup. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s noticeably slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. Restless, not really. It’s more like I feel slowed down. I know I’ve mentioned it, but even basic things at the clinic—getting wound supplies together or finishing charting—are taking me longer than they should. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s like I’m moving through syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're pacing back and forth or can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m pacing or unable to sit still. It’s kind of the opposite, honestly… everything feels slowed down. Like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic, like getting wound supplies together or finishing charting, take me noticeably longer than they should. I’m still trying to keep up, but… yeah, it’s slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, even when there's no need to?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I don’t feel like I have to keep moving. If anything, it’s the opposite. I’m just slower than I should be, I guess. Like at the clinic, even getting wound supplies together or finishing charting takes me longer than normal. It feels like I’m moving through syrup, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really been tracking my weight closely, so I don’t want to overstate it. But my appetite’s definitely been off. I know I should be eating regularly, especially with clinic shifts, but a lot of days food just doesn’t sound good, and then I’ll realize halfway through the day I’ve only had coffee or some crackers. I know that’s not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like work or reading lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I’m still getting things done, I think, but it’s taking more effort than it should. At work I keep rereading medication orders or wound-care notes because it feels like my brain is foggy, and I don’t want to miss anything. \n\nAnd decisions that used to be pretty automatic for me… I’ll kind of freeze and second-guess myself. It’s embarrassing to say, because I know I should be able to handle those things, but yeah, concentration has been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or jittery inside, even if you're not showing it outwardly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a jittery way. I mean, I get worried about things sometimes, like anybody would, but I haven’t noticed that kind of keyed-up feeling inside. It’s more like… I’m just trying to get through things, if that makes sense. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling this heavy sadness or pressure?\n\nPatient: Most days, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but for the past few weeks it’s been there almost every day… like this heavy sad pressure sitting on me most of the day.\n\nEven at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I know I should be eating regularly, especially with working at the clinic and everything. But most days food just doesn’t sound that good. I’ll get halfway through a shift and realize I’ve only had coffee or maybe some crackers all day. I know that’s not great… I’m trying to be better about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I mean, I don’t want to scare anyone, and I’m not saying I have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment was that I’ve had thoughts like, I wish I just wouldn’t wake up. Or like I could just not be here for a while. I know that sounds awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m speeding up, no. If anything it’s kind of the opposite… like everything takes more effort than it should. I’m still doing what I need to do, but at the clinic even simple stuff—getting wound care supplies together, finishing charting—feels like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s embarrassing, because I know I’m capable of doing it faster. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say worthless. That sounds so harsh. I do feel bad about not keeping up the way I think I should, but I try not to go down that road too much. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, I don’t think. It’s more… slowed down, if that makes sense. Like everything I do takes longer than it should. Even at the clinic, simple things like gathering wound supplies or finishing charting just feel like I’m moving through syrup. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s noticeably slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. Restless, not really. It’s more like I feel slowed down. I know I’ve mentioned it, but even basic things at the clinic—getting wound supplies together or finishing charting—are taking me longer than they should. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s like I’m moving through syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're pacing back and forth or can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m pacing or unable to sit still. It’s kind of the opposite, honestly… everything feels slowed down. Like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic, like getting wound supplies together or finishing charting, take me noticeably longer than they should. I’m still trying to keep up, but… yeah, it’s slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, even when there's no need to?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I don’t feel like I have to keep moving. If anything, it’s the opposite. I’m just slower than I should be, I guess. Like at the clinic, even getting wound supplies together or finishing charting takes me longer than normal. It feels like I’m moving through syrup, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really been tracking my weight closely, so I don’t want to overstate it. But my appetite’s definitely been off. I know I should be eating regularly, especially with clinic shifts, but a lot of days food just doesn’t sound good, and then I’ll realize halfway through the day I’ve only had coffee or some crackers. I know that’s not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like work or reading lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I’m still getting things done, I think, but it’s taking more effort than it should. At work I keep rereading medication orders or wound-care notes because it feels like my brain is foggy, and I don’t want to miss anything. \n\nAnd decisions that used to be pretty automatic for me… I’ll kind of freeze and second-guess myself. It’s embarrassing to say, because I know I should be able to handle those things, but yeah, concentration has been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or jittery inside, even if you're not showing it outwardly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a jittery way. I mean, I get worried about things sometimes, like anybody would, but I haven’t noticed that kind of keyed-up feeling inside. It’s more like… I’m just trying to get through things, if that makes sense. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling this heavy sadness or pressure?\n\nPatient: Most days, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but for the past few weeks it’s been there almost every day… like this heavy sad pressure sitting on me most of the day.\n\nEven at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find some interest in or enjoy, even if it's not as much as before?\n\nPatient: I mean… a little, maybe? I still try to act like I’m interested in things, but it’s not the same.\n\nAfter my shifts I used to paint sometimes, or go to yoga or salsa just to decompress. Lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch. If people text me about going out or doing something, I kind of… ignore it, which I know isn’t great. It just feels like too much effort, or like it won’t really be worth it anyway.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know that’s not great… I’m trying to be better about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I mean, I don’t want to scare anyone, and I’m not saying I have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment was that I’ve had thoughts like, I wish I just wouldn’t wake up. Or like I could just not be here for a while. I know that sounds awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m speeding up, no. If anything it’s kind of the opposite… like everything takes more effort than it should. I’m still doing what I need to do, but at the clinic even simple stuff—getting wound care supplies together, finishing charting—feels like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s embarrassing, because I know I’m capable of doing it faster. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say worthless. That sounds so harsh. I do feel bad about not keeping up the way I think I should, but I try not to go down that road too much. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, I don’t think. It’s more… slowed down, if that makes sense. Like everything I do takes longer than it should. Even at the clinic, simple things like gathering wound supplies or finishing charting just feel like I’m moving through syrup. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s noticeably slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. Restless, not really. It’s more like I feel slowed down. I know I’ve mentioned it, but even basic things at the clinic—getting wound supplies together or finishing charting—are taking me longer than they should. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s like I’m moving through syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're pacing back and forth or can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m pacing or unable to sit still. It’s kind of the opposite, honestly… everything feels slowed down. Like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic, like getting wound supplies together or finishing charting, take me noticeably longer than they should. I’m still trying to keep up, but… yeah, it’s slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, even when there's no need to?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I don’t feel like I have to keep moving. If anything, it’s the opposite. I’m just slower than I should be, I guess. Like at the clinic, even getting wound supplies together or finishing charting takes me longer than normal. It feels like I’m moving through syrup, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really been tracking my weight closely, so I don’t want to overstate it. But my appetite’s definitely been off. I know I should be eating regularly, especially with clinic shifts, but a lot of days food just doesn’t sound good, and then I’ll realize halfway through the day I’ve only had coffee or some crackers. I know that’s not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like work or reading lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I’m still getting things done, I think, but it’s taking more effort than it should. At work I keep rereading medication orders or wound-care notes because it feels like my brain is foggy, and I don’t want to miss anything. \n\nAnd decisions that used to be pretty automatic for me… I’ll kind of freeze and second-guess myself. It’s embarrassing to say, because I know I should be able to handle those things, but yeah, concentration has been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or jittery inside, even if you're not showing it outwardly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a jittery way. I mean, I get worried about things sometimes, like anybody would, but I haven’t noticed that kind of keyed-up feeling inside. It’s more like… I’m just trying to get through things, if that makes sense. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling this heavy sadness or pressure?\n\nPatient: Most days, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but for the past few weeks it’s been there almost every day… like this heavy sad pressure sitting on me most of the day.\n\nEven at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find some interest in or enjoy, even if it's not as much as before?\n\nPatient: I mean… a little, maybe? I still try to act like I’m interested in things, but it’s not the same.\n\nAfter my shifts I used to paint sometimes, or go to yoga or salsa just to decompress. Lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch. If people text me about going out or doing something, I kind of… ignore it, which I know isn’t great. It just feels like too much effort, or like it won’t really be worth it anyway.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no… if anything it’s kind of the opposite.\n\nI feel slowed down, like everything takes more effort than it should. Even at the clinic, things that should be quick—like gathering wound supplies or finishing charting—take me longer than they used to. It’s like I’m moving through syrup or something. I’m still getting things done, I think, just… noticeably slower. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch. Even answering texts feels like a lot, and I hate admitting that because it sounds bad, but it’s like… the pleasure isn’t really there enough to make it feel worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any issues with your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know, maybe a little, but I haven’t really been tracking it. Some nights feel better than others. I’m still getting to work, so I guess it’s not completely out of control or anything. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I know I should be eating regularly, especially with working at the clinic and everything. But most days food just doesn’t sound that good. I’ll get halfway through a shift and realize I’ve only had coffee or maybe some crackers all day. I know that’s not great… I’m trying to be better about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to be alive or wishing you could just disappear?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. I mean, I don’t want to scare anyone, and I’m not saying I have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But… what scared me enough to say something at my prenatal appointment was that I’ve had thoughts like, I wish I just wouldn’t wake up. Or like I could just not be here for a while. I know that sounds awful.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like you're speeding up in your thoughts or actions lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m speeding up, no. If anything it’s kind of the opposite… like everything takes more effort than it should. I’m still doing what I need to do, but at the clinic even simple stuff—getting wound care supplies together, finishing charting—feels like I’m moving through syrup or something. It’s embarrassing, because I know I’m capable of doing it faster. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about things or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say worthless. That sounds so harsh. I do feel bad about not keeping up the way I think I should, but I try not to go down that road too much. I’m not sure if that’s what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, I don’t think. It’s more… slowed down, if that makes sense. Like everything I do takes longer than it should. Even at the clinic, simple things like gathering wound supplies or finishing charting just feel like I’m moving through syrup. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s noticeably slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or irritable lately?\n\nPatient: Irritable… maybe a little, but I don’t think that’s the main thing. Restless, not really. It’s more like I feel slowed down. I know I’ve mentioned it, but even basic things at the clinic—getting wound supplies together or finishing charting—are taking me longer than they should. I’m trying to keep up, but it’s like I’m moving through syrup.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're pacing back and forth or can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m pacing or unable to sit still. It’s kind of the opposite, honestly… everything feels slowed down. Like I’m moving through syrup. Even simple things at the clinic, like getting wound supplies together or finishing charting, take me noticeably longer than they should. I’m still trying to keep up, but… yeah, it’s slower than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something, even when there's no need to?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I don’t feel like I have to keep moving. If anything, it’s the opposite. I’m just slower than I should be, I guess. Like at the clinic, even getting wound supplies together or finishing charting takes me longer than normal. It feels like I’m moving through syrup, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I haven’t really been tracking my weight closely, so I don’t want to overstate it. But my appetite’s definitely been off. I know I should be eating regularly, especially with clinic shifts, but a lot of days food just doesn’t sound good, and then I’ll realize halfway through the day I’ve only had coffee or some crackers. I know that’s not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating on things like work or reading lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a bit. I mean, I’m still getting things done, I think, but it’s taking more effort than it should. At work I keep rereading medication orders or wound-care notes because it feels like my brain is foggy, and I don’t want to miss anything. \n\nAnd decisions that used to be pretty automatic for me… I’ll kind of freeze and second-guess myself. It’s embarrassing to say, because I know I should be able to handle those things, but yeah, concentration has been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or jittery inside, even if you're not showing it outwardly?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a jittery way. I mean, I get worried about things sometimes, like anybody would, but I haven’t noticed that kind of keyed-up feeling inside. It’s more like… I’m just trying to get through things, if that makes sense. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: How many days a week would you say you've been feeling this heavy sadness or pressure?\n\nPatient: Most days, honestly. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but for the past few weeks it’s been there almost every day… like this heavy sad pressure sitting on me most of the day.\n\nEven at work, I can smile and talk to patients like I’m okay, but inside I feel like I’m barely holding myself together.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find some interest in or enjoy, even if it's not as much as before?\n\nPatient: I mean… a little, maybe? I still try to act like I’m interested in things, but it’s not the same.\n\nAfter my shifts I used to paint sometimes, or go to yoga or salsa just to decompress. Lately I mostly just go home and sit on the couch. If people text me about going out or doing something, I kind of… ignore it, which I know isn’t great. It just feels like too much effort, or like it won’t really be worth it anyway.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Have you been fidgeting or feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no… if anything it’s kind of the opposite.\n\nI feel slowed down, like everything takes more effort than it should. Even at the clinic, things that should be quick—like gathering wound supplies or finishing charting—take me longer than they used to. It’s like I’m moving through syrup or something. I’m still getting things done, I think, just… noticeably slower. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would you say this heavy sadness or pressure is interfering with your ability to function at work or in your daily life most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it is, more than I wanted to admit.\n\nI’m still showing up to work and getting through my shifts, so it’s not like I’ve completely fallen apart or anything, but my supervisor has noticed that some of my charting hasn’t been finished, which was embarrassing. And outside of work I’ve been kind of pulling back from friends and not doing the things that usually help keep me steady.\n\nI guess I keep telling myself I’m managing, but… it is affecting things. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
